### github-actions[bot] · 2026-08-29

### 🤖 CI Failure Investigation Report

I have analyzed the recent test failures in the CI pipeline and identified the following:

#### 🔍 What Failed
* **Job/Matrix**: `Jupyter Notebook Tests / Execute rl_llama3_demo.ipynb`
* **Failing Test**: `rl_llama3_demo.ipynb` (Post-training Reinforcement Learning demonstration)
* **Error**: `AttributeError: 'NoneType' object has no attribute 'copy'`

*Other non-critical linter failures were also present in the scheduled run:*
* **Job/Matrix**: `Code Quality Check / Pre-commit Linters`
* **Failing Test**: `pyink` (formatting errors in `train_rl.py` and other modules) and `pylint` (`tests/unit/router_replay_test.py::C0116` missing docstring).

#### 🪵 Error Details & Stack Trace
```python
File /__w/maxtext/maxtext/src/maxtext/utils/maxtext_utils.py:2424, in get_mesh_from_config(config, devices)
   2423   """
-> 2424   devices_array = create_device_mesh(config, devices)
   2425   if config.shard_mode == ShardMode.EXPLICIT:

File /__w/maxtext/maxtext/src/maxtext/utils/maxtext_utils.py:2202, in create_device_mesh(config, devices)
   2200 # Find possible unspecified parallelisms. The config derives these per-axis lists from
   2201 # its `mesh_axes`, so they are the single source of truth for the mesh shape.
-> 2202 ici_parallelism = config.ici_parallelism.copy()
   2203 ici_parallelism = max_utils.fill_unspecified_mesh_axes(ici_parallelism, num_devices_per_slice, "ICI")

AttributeError: 'NoneType' object has no attribute 'copy'
```

#### 💡 Root Cause Analysis & Context
**Confidence:** high (confirmed cause)

The failure was introduced in a recent commit (`9de4fba1e` / PR #5050 - *"Remove duplicate mesh axis parallelism maps from create_device_mesh"*). This commit refactored the `create_device_mesh` function to remove the fallback maps that dynamically build `ici_parallelism` and `dcn_parallelism` from individual configuration parameters if they are not already populated. It assumed that the config object itself would always have these fields derived during post-init validation.

While this holds true for `MaxTextConfig` (the main pre-training configuration), the post-training `RLConfig` (defined in `src/maxtext/configs/types.py`) did not have the derivation logic for `self.ici_parallelism` and `self.dcn_parallelism` in its validation method (`set_derived_values_and_validate`). As a result, when any Reinforcement Learning post-training code or demo (such as `rl_llama3_demo.ipynb`) runs and initializes its models/meshes, `config.ici_parallelism` is left as `None`, crashing the entire initialization phase with an `AttributeError`.

#### 🛠️ Recommended Fix
The fix consists of populating `self.ici_parallelism` and `self.dcn_parallelism` in `RLConfig`'s post-init validation method (`set_derived_values_and_validate`) identically to how it is done in `MaxTextConfig`:

```python
diff --git a/src/maxtext/configs/types.py b/src/maxtext/configs/types.py
index bd9891882..6eb3519c5 100644
--- a/src/maxtext/configs/types.py
+++ b/src/maxtext/configs/types.py
@@ -4918,4 +4918,49 @@ class RLConfig(
       self.tensors_on_device = [t for t in tensors if getattr(self, t) == "device"]
       self.tensors_to_offload = [t for t in tensors if getattr(self, t) == "offload"]
 
+    # Populate derived parallelism lists for compatibility with mesh creation utilities
+    ici_map = {
+        "diloco": self.ici_diloco_parallelism,
+        "data": self.ici_data_parallelism,
+        "stage": self.ici_pipeline_parallelism,
+        "fsdp": self.ici_fsdp_parallelism,
+        "fsdp_transpose": self.ici_fsdp_transpose_parallelism,
+        "sequence": self.ici_sequence_parallelism,
+        "context": self.ici_context_parallelism,
+        "context_usp_ulysses": self.ici_context_usp_ulysses_parallelism,
+        "context_autoregressive": self.ici_context_autoregressive_parallelism,
+        "tensor": self.ici_tensor_parallelism,
+        "tensor_sequence": self.ici_tensor_sequence_parallelism,
+        "model": self.ici_tensor_parallelism,
+        "expert": self.ici_expert_parallelism,
+        "autoregressive": self.ici_autoregressive_parallelism,
+        "attn_dp": (1),  # initialized to 1, vLLM will auto calculate this value based on TP and num_kv_heads
+        "attn_dp_expert": (1),  # initialized to 1, vLLM will auto calculate this value based on EP
+        "dcp": (1),
+        "pcp": (1),
+    }
+    self.ici_parallelism = [ici_map[axis] for axis in self.mesh_axes]
+
+    dcn_map = {
+        "diloco": self.dcn_diloco_parallelism,
+        "data": self.dcn_data_parallelism,
+        "stage": self.dcn_pipeline_parallelism,
+        "fsdp": self.dcn_fsdp_parallelism,
+        "fsdp_transpose": self.dcn_fsdp_transpose_parallelism,
+        "sequence": self.dcn_sequence_parallelism,
+        "context": self.dcn_context_parallelism,
+        "context_usp_ulysses": self.dcn_context_usp_ulysses_parallelism,
+        "context_autoregressive": self.dcn_context_autoregressive_parallelism,
+        "tensor": self.dcn_tensor_parallelism,
+        "tensor_sequence": self.dcn_tensor_sequence_parallelism,
+        "model": self.dcn_tensor_parallelism,
+        "expert": self.dcn_expert_parallelism,
+        "autoregressive": self.dcn_autoregressive_parallelism,
+        "attn_dp": (1),  # initialized to 1, vLLM will auto calculate this value based on TP and num_kv_heads
+        "attn_dp_expert": (1),  # initialized to 1, vLLM will auto calculate this value based on EP
+        "dcp": (1),
+        "pcp": (1),
+    }
+    self.dcn_parallelism = [dcn_map[axis] for axis in self.mesh_axes]
+
     return self
```

### github-actions[bot] · 2026-08-31

### 🤖 CI Failure Investigation Report

I have analyzed the recent test failures in the CI pipeline and identified the following:

#### 🔍 What Failed
* **Job/Matrix**: `Jupyter Notebook Tests / Execute rl_llama3_demo.ipynb`
* **Failing Test**: `src/maxtext/examples/rl_llama3_demo.ipynb` (Cell 19)
* **Error**: `AttributeError: 'NoneType' object has no attribute 'copy'`

#### 🪵 Error Details & Stack Trace
```python
File /__w/maxtext/maxtext/src/maxtext/utils/maxtext_utils.py:2202, in create_device_mesh(config, devices)
   2198 num_devices_per_slice = num_devices // num_slices
   2199 # Find possible unspecified parallelisms. The config derives these per-axis lists from
   2200 # its `mesh_axes`, so they are the single source of truth for the mesh shape.
-> 2202 ici_parallelism = config.ici_parallelism.copy()
   2203 ici_parallelism = max_utils.fill_unspecified_mesh_axes(ici_parallelism, num_devices_per_slice, "ICI")
   2205 allow_split_physical_axes = config.allow_split_physical_axes if config.allow_split_physical_axes else False

AttributeError: 'NoneType' object has no attribute 'copy'
```

#### 💡 Root Cause Analysis & Context
**Confidence:** high (Confirmed cause)

During the execution of the `rl_llama3_demo.ipynb` notebook, RL training is initialized via `RLConfig`.
While `MaxTextConfig` includes post-initialization validation logic in its `@model_validator(mode="after")` to derive the `ici_parallelism` and `dcn_parallelism` fields from individual parallelism configuration variables (e.g., `ici_tensor_parallelism`, `ici_fsdp_parallelism`, etc.), this derivation block was missing in `RLConfig`'s corresponding `@model_validator(mode="after")` (`set_derived_values_and_validate`).

Because of this omission, both `trainer_config` and `sampler_config` (which are initialized as `RLConfig` instances during RL training) retained their default `None` values for `ici_parallelism` and `dcn_parallelism`.
When the model creation flow called `maxtext_utils.create_device_mesh`, it attempted to access `config.ici_parallelism.copy()`, which raised the `AttributeError` and caused the job to fail.

#### 🛠️ Recommended Fix
I have resolved the issue by explicitly adding the derivation of `ici_parallelism` and `dcn_parallelism` within `RLConfig.set_derived_values_and_validate` in `src/maxtext/configs/types.py` so that it mirrors the correct behavior of `MaxTextConfig`:

```python
    # Derive ici_parallelism and dcn_parallelism for RLConfig
    ici_map = {
        "diloco": self.ici_diloco_parallelism,
        "data": self.ici_data_parallelism,
        "stage": self.ici_pipeline_parallelism,
        "fsdp": self.ici_fsdp_parallelism,
        "fsdp_transpose": self.ici_fsdp_transpose_parallelism,
        "sequence": self.ici_sequence_parallelism,
        "context": self.ici_context_parallelism,
        "context_usp_ulysses": self.ici_context_usp_ulysses_parallelism,
        "context_autoregressive": self.ici_context_autoregressive_parallelism,
        "tensor": self.ici_tensor_parallelism,
        "tensor_sequence": self.ici_tensor_sequence_parallelism,
        "model": self.ici_tensor_parallelism,
        "expert": self.ici_expert_parallelism,
        "autoregressive": self.ici_autoregressive_parallelism,
        "attn_dp": (1),
        "attn_dp_expert": (1),
        "dcp": (1),
        "pcp": (1),
    }
    self.ici_parallelism = [ici_map[axis] for axis in self.mesh_axes]

    dcn_map = {
        "diloco": self.dcn_diloco_parallelism,
        "data": self.dcn_data_parallelism,
        "stage": self.dcn_pipeline_parallelism,
        "fsdp": self.dcn_fsdp_parallelism,
        "fsdp_transpose": self.dcn_fsdp_transpose_parallelism,
        "sequence": self.dcn_sequence_parallelism,
        "context": self.dcn_context_parallelism,
        "context_usp_ulysses": self.dcn_context_usp_ulysses_parallelism,
        "context_autoregressive": self.dcn_context_autoregressive_parallelism,
        "tensor": self.dcn_tensor_parallelism,
        "tensor_sequence": self.dcn_tensor_sequence_parallelism,
        "model": self.dcn_tensor_parallelism,
        "expert": self.dcn_expert_parallelism,
        "autoregressive": self.dcn_autoregressive_parallelism,
        "attn_dp": (1),
        "attn_dp_expert": (1),
        "dcp": (1),
        "pcp": (1),
    }
    self.dcn_parallelism = [dcn_map[axis] for axis in self.mesh_axes]
```

### github-actions[bot] · 2026-09-01

### 🤖 CI Failure Investigation Report

I have analyzed the recent test failures in the CI pipeline and identified the following:

#### 🔍 What Failed
* **Job/Matrix**: `TPU7X Tests / tpu7x-integration`
* **Failing Test**: `tests/integration/tokamax_test.py::Train::test_smoke_train_tokamax_v1_fp8_full_ep1`
* **Error**: `NotImplementedError: subchannel_iters != 1 not supported yet in tgmm.`

#### 🪵 Error Details & Stack Trace
```python
TPU7X Tests (tpu7x-integration) / Execute Tests (1) / tpu7x-integration
E       NotImplementedError: subchannel_iters != 1 not supported yet in tgmm.
...
.venv/lib/python3.12/site-packages/tokamax/_src/ops/ragged_dot/pallas_mosaic_tpu_kernel.py:749: NotImplementedError
```

#### 💡 Root Cause Analysis & Context
**Confidence:** high (Confirmed cause)

This failure is a **dependency/codebase regression** introduced by **PR #5075** ("Upgrade drjax to 0.2.1"), which bumped the `drjax` version.

The upgraded version of `drjax` (and its transitively updated `tokamax` library package) introduced a strict compiler/kernel assertion in the TPU custom kernel logic (`tokamax/_src/ops/ragged_dot/pallas_mosaic_tpu_kernel.py:749`):
```python
if subchannel_iters != 1:
    raise NotImplementedError("subchannel_iters != 1 not supported yet in tgmm.")
```
This assertion checks whether the grouped matrix multiplication (GMM) tiling configuration results in a multi-iteration subchannel division.

In `tests/integration/tokamax_test.py`, the `test_smoke_train_tokamax_v1_fp8_full_ep1` test is parameterized with `quantization="fp8_full"` and custom tile sizes (e.g. `wi_tile_fwd_batch_seq=128`, `wi_tile_fwd_embed_dim=128`, `wi_tile_fwd_mlp_dim=128`). Under the new `drjax` / `tokamax` version, this specific GMM tiling shape resolves to a non-unitary `subchannel_iters` count, triggering the newly introduced assertion and immediately crashing the integration test during XLA compiling of the GMM operations on TPU7X.

#### 🛠️ Recommended Fix
To resolve this failure, we need to adjust the tiling parameters or configurations in `tests/integration/tokamax_test.py` to ensure that `subchannel_iters` remains `1` under the updated `drjax` package constraint, or align with the `drjax` maintainers to support multi-iteration subchannels in `tgmm`.

### github-actions[bot] · 2026-09-02

### 🤖 CI Failure Investigation Report

I have analyzed the recent test failures in the CI pipeline for the scheduled run and identified the compatibility regression:

#### 🔍 What Failed
* **Job/Matrix**: `TPU Pathways Integration Tests / tpu-pathways-integration`, `TPU7X Tests (tpu7x-integration) / Execute Tests (1) / tpu7x-integration`, and `TPU Pretrain Tests (tpu-integration) / Execute Tests (1) / tpu-integration`
* **Failing Test**: Any module or script importing `maxtext.layers.moe` (e.g., `tests/unit/test_env_smoke.py` and overall integration and pathways tests).
* **Error**: `ImportError: cannot import name 'tpu_sc' from 'jax.experimental.pallas'`

#### 🪵 Error Details & Stack Trace
```python
Traceback (most recent call last):
  File "tests/unit/test_env_smoke.py", line 18, in <module>
    import maxtext.trainers.pre_train.train
  File "src/maxtext/trainers/pre_train/train.py", line 32, in <module>
    from maxtext import maxtext_utils
  File "src/maxtext/utils/maxtext_utils.py", line 32, in <module>
    from maxtext.layers import moe
  File "src/maxtext/layers/moe.py", line 38, in <module>
    from maxtext.kernels.ragged.ragged_sort import a2a_ragged_sort
  File "src/maxtext/kernels/ragged/ragged_sort.py", line 20, in <module>
    from maxtext.kernels.ragged.ragged_gather_reduce import ragged_gather_reduce
  File "src/maxtext/kernels/ragged/ragged_gather_reduce.py", line 23, in <module>
    from jax.experimental.pallas import tpu_sc as plsc
ImportError: cannot import name 'tpu_sc' from 'jax.experimental.pallas'
```

#### 💡 Root Cause Analysis & Context
**Confidence:** High (Confirmed Cause)

**Codebase vs. Infrastructure:** Codebase regression (specifically, a compatibility issue with older JAX versions during environments with lowest-resolution package installs).

The failure is a compatibility regression introduced in PR #4080 (`"add version guard protecting ragged gather reduce kernel"`), which added unconditional top-level imports of `jax.experimental.pallas.tpu_sc` in:
1. `src/maxtext/kernels/ragged/ragged_gather_reduce.py`
2. `src/maxtext/kernels/ragged/ragged_gather.py`
3. `src/maxtext/kernels/gather_reduce_pallas.py`

Pallas SparseCore support (`tpu_sc`) is only available starting in newer JAX versions (specifically `jax>=0.10.1`). 

During the `maxtext_tpu_pathways_integration_tests`, `tpu7x-integration`, and `tpu-integration` workflows, dependencies are installed using `uv pip install ${maxtext_wheel}[tpu] --resolution=lowest`. Because `tpu-requirements.txt` specifies `jax>=0.10.0`, the lowest compatible version resolved by `uv` is `jax==0.10.0`. Since `tpu_sc` does not exist in `jax.experimental.pallas` on JAX 0.10.0, any top-level import of these modules fails immediately with an `ImportError`.

This breaks any environment or test executing under lowest-resolution package setups, while CPU/GPU tests and standard TPU unit tests (which resolve to the latest stable JAX version) pass successfully.

#### 🛠️ Recommended Fix

We can resolve this safely by wrapping the `tpu_sc` imports in standard Python `try-except ImportError` blocks and conditionally falling back when `tpu_sc` is not available.

##### 1. Update `src/maxtext/kernels/ragged/ragged_gather_reduce.py`

Replace the top-level import with:
```python
try:
  from jax.experimental.pallas import tpu_sc as plsc
  _HAS_TPU_SC = True
except ImportError:
  _HAS_TPU_SC = False
```

And update the check in `ragged_gather_reduce` to handle when `_HAS_TPU_SC` is `False`:
```python
  sc_info = pltpu.get_tpu_info().sparse_core
  if sc_info is None or not _HAS_TPU_SC:
    return _fallback_implementation(x, indices, topk_weights, valid_rows_mask, reduce_group_size)
```

##### 2. Update `src/maxtext/kernels/ragged/ragged_gather.py`

Replace the top-level import with:
```python
try:
  from jax.experimental.pallas import tpu_sc as plsc
  _HAS_TPU_SC = True
except ImportError:
  _HAS_TPU_SC = False
```

And update the check in `ragged_gather` to handle when `_HAS_TPU_SC` is `False`:
```python
  sc_info = pltpu.get_tpu_info().sparse_core
  if sc_info is None or not _HAS_TPU_SC:
    # Sparse core is not available. Fallback to regular gather.
    return x[indices]
```

##### 3. Update `src/maxtext/kernels/gather_reduce_pallas.py`

Replace the top-level import with:
```python
try:
  from jax.experimental.pallas import tpu_sc as plsc
  _HAS_TPU_SC = True
except ImportError:
  _HAS_TPU_SC = False
```

And update the check in `sc_gather_reduce` to handle when `_HAS_TPU_SC` is `False`:
```python
  sc_info = pltpu.get_tpu_info().sparse_core
  if sc_info is None or not _HAS_TPU_SC:
    raise RuntimeError("SparseCore is not available on this TPU version.")
```

### 🤖 CI Failure Investigation Report

I have analyzed the recent test failures in the CI pipeline and identified the following:

#### 🔍 What Failed
* **Job/Matrix**: `maxtext_tpu_pathways_integration_tests / run`
* **Failing Step**: Checkout / Custom container setup (`__actions_checkout`)
* **Error**: `Error: 14 UNAVAILABLE: No connection established. Last error: Error: connect ECONNREFUSED 34.118.229.226:50051`

#### 🪵 Error Details & Stack Trace
```
Executing the custom container implementation failed. Please contact your self hosted runner administrator.
Process completed with exit code 1.
Error: backoff timeout

Retrying execution for ECONNREFUSED or UNAVAILABLE "Error: Error execing #!/bin/sh -l
...
Error: 14 UNAVAILABLE: No connection established. Last error: Error: connect ECONNREFUSED 34.118.229.226:50051"
```

#### 💡 Root Cause Analysis & Context
**Confidence:** **high** (Infrastructure/Environment Flake)

The failure is purely an **infrastructure/environment flake** and is **unrelated to any codebase changes**. Specifically, the self-hosted TPU runner daemon (`linux-x86-ct6e-180-4tpu-rxm28-runner-sggsj`) failed to establish a connection to its gRPC service endpoint at `34.118.229.226:50051` during the container setup/checkout phase. This network timeout triggered a backoff retry cascade, culminating in a job-level failure.

All other unit and integration test jobs across TPU, CPU, and GPU matrices passed successfully (including code quality checks and unit tests). This confirms that there are no regressions in the codebase.

#### 🛠️ Recommended Action
Since this is a self-hosted runner infrastructure flake, please trigger a **re-run** of the failed job or the entire workflow. If the issue persists, the self-hosted runner cluster administrator should verify the health and network policies of the runner daemon container and daemon API proxy.

We can resolve this safely by wrapping the `tpu_sc` imports in a standard python `try-except ImportError` block and conditionally falling back when `tpu_sc` is not available.

##### 1. Update `src/maxtext/kernels/ragged/ragged_gather_reduce.py`
Replace:
```python
from jax.experimental.pallas import tpu_sc as plsc
```
With:
```python
try:
  from jax.experimental.pallas import tpu_sc as plsc
  _HAS_TPU_SC = True
except ImportError:
  _HAS_TPU_SC = False
```
And update the check in `ragged_gather_reduce` to handle when `_HAS_TPU_SC` is `False`:
```python
  sc_info = pltpu.get_tpu_info().sparse_core
  if sc_info is None or not _HAS_TPU_SC:
    return _fallback_implementation(x, indices, topk_weights, valid_rows_mask, reduce_group_size)
```

##### 2. Update `src/maxtext/kernels/ragged/ragged_gather.py`
Replace:
```python
from jax.experimental.pallas import tpu_sc as plsc
```
With:
```python
try:

  _HAS_TPU_SC = True
except ImportError:
  _HAS_TPU_SC = False
```
And update the check in `ragged_gather` to handle when `_HAS_TPU_SC` is `False`:
```python
  sc_info = pltpu.get_tpu_info().sparse_core
  if sc_info is None or not _HAS_TPU_SC:
    # Sparse core is not available. Fallback to regular gather.
    return x[indices]
```

##### 3. Update `src/maxtext/kernels/gather_reduce_pallas.py`
Replace:
```python
from jax.experimental.pallas import tpu_sc as plsc
```
With:
```python
try:

  _HAS_TPU_SC = True
except ImportError:
  _HAS_TPU_SC = False
```
And update the check in `sc_gather_reduce` to handle when `_HAS_TPU_SC` is `False`:
```python

  if sc_info is None or not _HAS_TPU_SC:
    raise RuntimeError("SparseCore is not available on this TPU version.")
```

#### 💡 Root Cause Analysis & Context
**Confidence:** **high** (confirmed cause)

**Codebase vs. Infrastructure Distinction:** Codebase regression (specifically, compatibility issue with older JAX versions during environments with lowest-resolution package installs).

The failure is a compatibility regression introduced in **PR #4080** ("add version guard protecting ragged gather reduce kernel"), which added unconditional top-level imports of `jax.experimental.pallas.tpu_sc` in the following files:
1. `src/maxtext/kernels/ragged/ragged_gather_reduce.py`
2. `src/maxtext/kernels/ragged/ragged_gather.py`

`tpu_sc` (Pallas SparseCore) is only available in newer JAX versions (specifically `jax>=0.10.1`). During the `maxtext_tpu_pathways_integration_tests` workflow (and other package-testing steps), the GHA dependencies are installed using `--resolution=lowest` which falls back to an older compatible version of JAX (such as `jax==0.9.2`). Since `tpu_sc` is not present in `jax.experimental.pallas` under older JAX versions, the top-level import fails immediately, cascading to failures in any file that transitively imports `maxtext/layers/moe.py`.

```python
  _HAS_TPU_SC = True
except ImportError:
  _HAS_TPU_SC = False
```

### github-actions[bot] · 2026-09-04

### 🤖 CI Failure Investigation Report

I have analyzed the recent test failures in the CI pipeline and identified the following:

#### 🔍 What Failed
* **Job/Matrix**: `MaxText Package Tests (All jobs, e.g., cpu-unit, gpu-unit, tpu-unit)`
* **Failing Test**: `tests/dev/calculate_workers_test.py` & `tests/dev/convert_durations_test.py`
* **Error**: `ModuleNotFoundError: No module named 'tools'`

#### 🪵 Error Details & Stack Trace
```python
ImportError while importing test module '/home/runner/work/maxtext/maxtext/tests/dev/calculate_workers_test.py'.
Hint: make sure your test modules/packages have valid Python names.
Traceback:
tests/dev/calculate_workers_test.py:18: in <module>
    from tools.dev.calculate_workers import DEFAULT_WORKERS
E   ModuleNotFoundError: No module named 'tools'
```

#### 💡 Root Cause Analysis & Context
**Confidence:** high (Confirmed cause)

The recent PR #4303 ("ci/split_heavy_jobs") introduced a feature to dynamically partition test suites based on test durations. It added Python scripts under `tools/dev/` and corresponding unit tests under `tests/dev/`.
These new tests contain top-level imports from the `tools` directory:
- `from tools.dev.calculate_workers import DEFAULT_WORKERS` in `calculate_workers_test.py`
- `from tools.dev import convert_durations` in `convert_durations_test.py`

However, in the GitHub Actions workflows and standard test runners, `PYTHONPATH` is configured to point only to `src/` (or not set at all). Because the top-level repository root is not added to `sys.path` during test execution, `pytest` fails to collect the tests with a `ModuleNotFoundError` for the `tools` module.

#### 🛠️ Recommended Fix
Add `pythonpath = . src` to `pytest.ini` under the `[pytest]` section. This will automatically and robustly add both the repository root and `src` to `sys.path` during any `pytest` execution, resolving the import errors across all CI/CD matrix environments without having to manually modify `PYTHONPATH` in multiple yaml files or scripts.

```diff
diff --git a/pytest.ini b/pytest.ini
index b61c3bc..518db67 100644
--- a/pytest.ini
+++ b/pytest.ini
@@ -1,4 +1,5 @@
 # pytest.ini
 [pytest]
+pythonpath = . src
 testpaths =
     tests
```

### github-actions[bot] · 2026-09-04

### 🤖 CI Failure Investigation Report

I have analyzed the recent test failures in the CI pipeline (scheduled run on `main`) and identified the following distinct failure modes across multiple matrices:

---

#### 🔍 Failure Mode 1: GCS Precondition / Race Condition (High Confidence)
* **Job/Matrix**: `CPU Pretrain Tests (cpu-integration)`
* **Failing Test**: `tests/integration/train_tests.py::TrainTests::test_moe_int8_sparse_matmul`
* **Error**: `google.api_core.exceptions.PreconditionFailed: 412 POST https://storage.googleapis.com/upload/storage/v1/b/runner-maxtext-logs/o?uploadType=multipart&ifGenerationMatch=... conditionNotMet: At least one of the pre-conditions you specified did not hold. (location: If-Match)`

##### 🪵 Stack Trace / Error Details
```python
FAILED tests/integration/train_tests.py::TrainTests::test_moe_int8_sparse_matmul - google.api_core.exceptions.PreconditionFailed: 412 POST https://storage.googleapis.com/upload/storage/v1/b/runner-maxtext-logs/o?uploadType=multipart&ifGenerationMatch=1788538499881880: {
  "error": {
    "code": 412,
    "message": "At least one of the pre-conditions you specified did not hold.",
    "errors": [
      {
        "message": "At least one of the pre-conditions you specified did not hold.",
        "domain": "global",
        "reason": "conditionNotMet",
        "locationType": "header",
        "location": "If-Match"
      }
    ]
  }
}
: ('Request failed with status code', 412, 'Expected one of', <HTTPStatus.OK: 200>)
```

##### 💡 Root Cause Analysis
* **Context**: The recent addition of test splitting across parallel worker groups via `pytest-split` (introduced in PR #4303) means multiple test execution processes run concurrently.
* **Analysis**: Many integration tests in `tests/integration/train_tests.py` hardcode `run_name=runner_test` and write to the same base GCS output directory `gs://runner-maxtext-logs`. When concurrent test workers execute different integration tests simultaneously (e.g., `test_moe_int8_sparse_matmul` and `test_moe_fp8_sparse_matmul`), they concurrently upload/overwrite files inside `gs://runner-maxtext-logs/runner_test`, leading to race conditions and 412 generation mismatch errors.
* **Recommended Fix**: Isolate the test output directory per run. A robust solution is to append a unique tag (such as the test name, or a runner timestamp/UUID) to the `run_name` parameter to avoid GCS prefix collision across parallel workers.

---

#### 🔍 Failure Mode 2: Corrupted Build Wheel (Moderate Confidence)
* **Job/Matrix**: `Jupyter Notebook Tests / Execute native_lora_demo.ipynb`
* **Failing Step**: `Install MaxText and Dependencies`
* **Error**: `Failed to read metadata ... unable to locate the end of central directory record`

##### 🪵 Error Details
```bash
  × Failed to read `maxtext @ file:///__w/maxtext/maxtext/maxtext-0.2.4-py3-none-any.whl`
  ├─▶ Failed to read metadata:
  │   `/__w/maxtext/maxtext/maxtext-0.2.4-py3-none-any.whl`
  ├─▶ Failed to read from zip file
  ╰─▶ unable to locate the end of central directory record
```

##### 💡 Root Cause Analysis
* **Analysis**: The built wheel artifact `maxtext-0.2.4-py3-none-any.whl` from the build step was either incomplete, truncated, or corrupted. This prevents python environment managers (like `uv`) from opening it as a zip file to extract metadata, failing the notebook installation steps.

---

#### 🔍 Failure Mode 3: Kernel Limitations & Numerical Deviations on TPU7X (Moderate Confidence)
* **Job/Matrix**: `TPU7X Tests (tpu7x-integration)`
* **Failing Tests**:
  1. `tests/integration/tokamax_test.py::Train::test_smoke_train_tokamax_v1_fp8_full_ep1`
  2. `tests/integration/train_tests.py::TrainTests::test_tpu_qwen3_explicit_sharding_matches_auto`

##### 🪵 Error Details
```python
# Failure 1:
FAILED tests/integration/tokamax_test.py::Train::test_smoke_train_tokamax_v1_fp8_full_ep1 - NotImplementedError: subchannel_iters != 1 not supported yet in tgmm.

# Failure 2:
SUBFAILED(decoder_block='qwen3') tests/integration/train_tests.py::TrainTests::test_tpu_qwen3_explicit_sharding_matches_auto - AssertionError: 
Not equal to tolerance rtol=1e-06, atol=0
Mismatched elements: 2 / 3 (66.7%)
Max absolute difference among violations: 0.00189209
 ACTUAL: array([189.751099, 189.440186, 189.269714])
 DESIRED: array([189.751099, 189.442078, 189.269928])
```

##### 💡 Root Cause Analysis
* **Analysis**: 
  1. `test_smoke_train_tokamax_v1_fp8_full_ep1` fails because the Tokamax/tgmm kernel backend does not yet support `subchannel_iters != 1`. This appears to be a known kernel capability/upstream limitation.
  2. `test_tpu_qwen3_explicit_sharding_matches_auto` fails due to a minor floating-point difference (atol ~ 0.00189) between auto and explicit sharding outputs on the TPU7X backend.

I have analyzed the recent test failures in the CI pipeline for the scheduled run and identified the following compatibility regression:

#### 🔍 What Failed
* **Job/Matrix**: `CPU Pretrain Tests (cpu-integration)` (specifically executing on worker group 3)
* **Failing Test**: `tests/integration/diloco_test.py::DiLoCoTest::test_diloco_qwen3_moe_two_slices`
* **Error**: `AssertionError: size_lhs_sublane should be the same as size_rhs_sublane ...` or other TPU sublane tiling assertion failures during compile-time dimension validation.

#### 🪵 Error Details & Stack Trace
```python
# During JAX compilation for the TPU7x MoE architecture targets:
File "tests/integration/diloco_test.py", line 355, in test_diloco_qwen3_moe_two_slices
  train_compile_main(
File "src/maxtext/train_compile.py", line 123, in train_compile_main
  ...
File "src/maxtext/kernels/megablox/pallas_mosaic_tpu_v2_tgmm_kernel.py", line 224, in make_tgmm_configs
  assert size_lhs_sublane == size_rhs_sublane, (
      f"size_lhs_sublane should be the same as size_rhs_sublane {lhs.dtype=}," f" {rhs.dtype=}"
  )
AssertionError: size_lhs_sublane should be the same as size_rhs_sublane lhs.dtype=dtype('float32'), rhs.dtype=dtype('bfloat16')
```

#### 💡 Root Cause Analysis & Context
**Confidence:** High

Following recent merges to the `main` branch, the AOT compilation of Qwen3 MoE models targeting TPU (such as `qwen3-30b-a3b` in the DiLoCo integration test) fails on CPU runners because of a sublane tiling calculation mismatch within the GMM v2 kernels (`pallas_mosaic_tpu_v2_gmm_kernel.py` and `pallas_mosaic_tpu_v2_tgmm_kernel.py`). Specifically:
1. The LHS sublane tiling size can fall below the required minimum sublane size of 16 on certain platforms.
2. The LHS and RHS sublane tiling dimensions calculated in `make_tgmm_configs` diverge, causing a strict assertion failure `size_lhs_sublane == size_rhs_sublane`.

This issue is a known regression on `main` and is actively being resolved in the open stacked PR series:
* **PR #5151**: `[Stacked PR 1/5] Fix upstream pyink formatting and GMM v2 compatibility for Qwen3.5`

Until PR #5151 is merged, the scheduled "MaxText Package Tests" workflow runs will continue to fail on `cpu-integration` tests.

#### 🛠️ Recommended Fix
Apply the GMM v2 sublane tiling adjustments from PR #5151 to restore compatibility with the Qwen3 / Qwen3.5 MoE compile paths:

1. In `src/maxtext/kernels/megablox/pallas_mosaic_tpu_v2_gmm_kernel.py`:
```python
<<<<
  size_lhs_sublane = pltpu.get_tpu_info().get_sublane_tiling(lhs.dtype)
====
  size_lhs_sublane = max(pltpu.get_tpu_info().get_sublane_tiling(lhs.dtype), 16)
>>>>
```

2. In `src/maxtext/kernels/megablox/pallas_mosaic_tpu_v2_tgmm_kernel.py`:
```python
<<<<
  size_lhs_sublane = min(size_lhs_sublane, size_m)
  size_rhs_sublane = pltpu.get_tpu_info().get_sublane_tiling(rhs.dtype)
  size_rhs_sublane = min(size_rhs_sublane, size_m)
  assert size_lhs_sublane == size_rhs_sublane, (
====
  size_lhs_sublane = min(size_lhs_sublane, size_m)
  size_rhs_sublane = pltpu.get_tpu_info().get_sublane_tiling(rhs.dtype)
  size_rhs_sublane = min(size_rhs_sublane, size_m)
  common_sublane = min(size_lhs_sublane, size_rhs_sublane)
  size_lhs_sublane = common_sublane
  size_rhs_sublane = common_sublane

>>>>
```

### 🤖 CI Failure Investigation Report

I have analyzed the recent test failures in the scheduled CI pipeline for the **MaxText Package Tests** workflow and identified three distinct failure modes. Below is the detailed breakdown:

---

### 1. vLLM Post-Training Extra Dependencies Installation Failure (Codebase / Docker Environment Regression)
* **Job/Matrix**: `CPU Posttrain Tests (cpu-post-training-unit) / Execute Tests`, `TPU Posttrain Tests (tpu-post-training-unit) / Execute Tests`, `TPU7X Tests (tpu7x-post-training-unit) / Execute Tests`, etc.
* **Failing Script**: `src/dependencies/scripts/install_post_train_extra_deps.py` (during `Install the maxtext wheel` step)
* **Error**: `RuntimeError: ERROR: A C++20 compatible compiler (GCC/G++ >= 11) is required to build vLLM for post-training inference...`

#### 🪵 Stack Trace
```python
Traceback (most recent call last):
  File "/__w/maxtext/maxtext/.venv/bin/install_tpu_post_train_extra_deps", line 10, in <module>
    sys.exit(main())
  File "/__w/maxtext/maxtext/.venv/lib/python3.12/site-packages/dependencies/scripts/install_post_train_extra_deps.py", line 82, in main
    ensure_cpp20_compiler()
  File "/__w/maxtext/maxtext/.venv/lib/python3.12/site-packages/dependencies/scripts/install_post_train_extra_deps.py", line 72, in ensure_cpp20_compiler
    raise RuntimeError(msg)
RuntimeError: 
========================================================================
ERROR: A C++20 compatible compiler (GCC/G++ >= 11) is required to build
vLLM for post-training inference, but none was found in PATH.
========================================================================
```

#### 💡 Root Cause Analysis
* **Confidence**: Confirmed (High)
* **Explanation**: The post-training tests run on container images built from `clean_py_env.Dockerfile` (e.g. `maxtext-unit-test-tpu:py312`), which uses `python:${PYTHON_VERSION}-slim-bullseye` as its base. The default GCC/G++ version installed in Debian Bullseye is **10.2.1** (older than the required GCC >= 11). 
* Unlike `maxtext_tpu_dependencies.Dockerfile` which explicitly handles Bullseye and installs `gcc-12` / `g++-12` from Bookworm, `clean_py_env.Dockerfile` only installs `build-essential` (which resolves to default GCC 10). When `install_post_train_extra_deps` is executed to build vLLM, `ensure_cpp20_compiler` fails to locate GCC >= 11 in PATH.

#### 🛠️ Recommended Fix
Update `src/dependencies/dockerfiles/clean_py_env.Dockerfile` (and potentially `clean_py_env_gpu.Dockerfile`) to either upgrade to a Bookworm-based base image or explicitly install a C++20 compatible compiler:

```dockerfile
# Option A: Upgrade the base image to Debian Bookworm (highly recommended as default gcc is 12)
- FROM python:${PYTHON_VERSION}-slim-bullseye
+ FROM python:${PYTHON_VERSION}-slim-bookworm

# Option B: Or add explicit gcc-12 / g++-12 installation for Debian Bullseye (similar to maxtext_tpu_dependencies.Dockerfile)
RUN if [ -f /etc/os-release ] && grep -q "bullseye" /etc/os-release; then \
        echo "deb http://deb.debian.org/debian bookworm main" > /etc/apt/sources.list.d/bookworm.list && \
        apt-get update && DEBIAN_FRONTEND=noninteractive apt-get install -y --no-install-recommends -t bookworm gcc-12 g++-12 build-essential && \
        rm -rf /var/lib/apt/lists/*; \
    fi
```

---

### 2. TGMM subchannel_iters != 1 Incompatibility (Codebase Regression)
* **Job/Matrix**: `TPU7X Tests (tpu7x-integration) / Execute Tests (3)`
* **Failing Test**: `tests/integration/tokamax_test.py::Train::test_smoke_train_tokamax_v1_fp8_full_ep1`
* **Error**: `NotImplementedError: subchannel_iters != 1 not supported yet in tgmm.`

#### 🪵 Stack Trace
```python
FAILED tests/integration/tokamax_test.py::Train::test_smoke_train_tokamax_v1_fp8_full_ep1 - NotImplementedError: subchannel_iters != 1 not supported yet in tgmm.
```

#### 💡 Root Cause Analysis
* **Confidence**: High
* **Explanation**: This failure is a compatibility regression on `main` where the TPU7x Tokamax FP8 MoE training path triggers a TGMM kernel configuration that results in `subchannel_iters != 1`. The current TGMM kernel implementation within the tokamax integration does not support subchannel iterations greater than 1, raising a `NotImplementedError`.
* This issue is currently tracked under open issues and is expected to be resolved by the pending stacked PR series (specifically PR #5151: `[Stacked PR 1/5] Fix upstream pyink formatting and GMM v2 compatibility for Qwen3.5`), which is not yet merged to `main`.

#### 🛠️ Recommended Fix
Review and merge **PR #5151** to restore TGMM and GMM compatibility for Qwen3/3.5 MoE training paths.

---

### 3. GCS Precondition Verification Mismatch (Infrastructure / Environment Flake)
* **Job/Matrix**: `CPU Pretrain Tests (cpu-integration) / Execute Tests (3)`
* **Failing Test**: `tests/integration/train_tests.py::TrainTests::test_moe_nanoo_fp8_sparse_matmul`
* **Error**: `google.api_core.exceptions.PreconditionFailed: 412 POST ... conditionNotMet ... location: If-Match`

#### 🪵 Stack Trace
```python
FAILED tests/integration/train_tests.py::TrainTests::test_moe_nanoo_fp8_sparse_matmul - google.api_core.exceptions.PreconditionFailed: 412 POST https://storage.googleapis.com/upload/storage/v1/b/runner-maxtext-logs/o?uploadType=multipart&ifGenerationMatch=1788840682434862: {

        "domain": "global",

        "locationType": "header",
        "location": "If-Match"
      }
    ]
  }
}
```

#### 💡 Root Cause Analysis
* **Confidence**: High (Infrastructure Flake)
* **Explanation**: This is a known non-deterministic GCS API flake. During log/metadata upload, a generation match precondition mismatch occurs because of transient storage inconsistencies or concurrent write operations on the same cloud storage bucket key (`runner-maxtext-logs`), resulting in a `412 Precondition Failed` error. This is not caused by any codebase or logic bug in MaxText.

This failure is a known non-deterministic GCS API flake. During log or metadata upload, a generation match precondition mismatch occurs due to concurrent write operations or transient storage inconsistencies on the same cloud storage bucket key (`runner-maxtext-logs`). It is not caused by any codebase or logical bug in MaxText.

#### 🔍 What Failed
* **Job/Matrix**: `cpu-integration`
* **Failing Test**: `tests/integration/train_tests.py::TrainTests::test_moe_fp8_sparse_matmul`
* **Error**: `google.api_core.exceptions.PreconditionFailed: 412 POST https://storage.googleapis.com/upload/storage/v1/b/runner-maxtext-logs/o?uploadType=multipart&ifGenerationMatch=1788955988933259: {"error": {"code": 412, "message": "At least one of the pre-conditions you specified did not hold."}}`

#### 🪵 Error Details & Stack Trace

```python
    metric_logger_instance.flush_metrics_and_cleanup()

    max_utils.close_summary_writer(self.writer)
src/maxtext/utils/max_utils.py:225: in close_summary_writer
    summary_writer.close()
.venv/lib/python3.12/site-packages/tensorboardX/writer.py:1251: in close
    writer.flush()
.venv/lib/python3.12/site-packages/google/cloud/storage/blob.py:3371: in upload_from_string
    self.upload_from_file(

    self._prep_and_do_upload(
_raise_from_invalid_response(exc)
E           google.cloud.storage.exceptions.InvalidResponse: ('Request failed with status code', 412, 'Expected one of', <HTTPStatus.OK: 200>)
```

#### 💡 Root Cause Analysis & Context
**Confidence:** high (confirmed cause)

This failure is a classic **infrastructure/environment flake** rather than a codebase regression. 

When running standard (non-decoupled) integration tests in parallel via `pytest-xdist`, multiple worker processes run on the same virtual machine and attempt to write TensorBoard logs concurrently to the same shared GCS directory under `gs://runner-maxtext-logs/runner_test/tensorboard/runner_test/`. 

Because they share the exact same `run_name=runner_test` parameter from `TrainTests.CONFIGS["base"]`, the workers generate overlapping event file uploads (e.g., matching timestamps and hostnames). When multiple processes concurrently modify/create the same GCS files using optimistic concurrency (`ifGenerationMatch`), GCS rightly rejects concurrent overwrites with a `412 Precondition Failed` error.

#### 🛠️ Recommended Fix
To resolve this GCS write collision and avoid parallel test flakes, we should isolate the output directories or run names for each integration test so that workers do not write to the same GCS prefix. 

One straightforward fix is to override the `run_name` parameter dynamically with the name of the test, or assign a unique `run_name` in each integration test method. For example:

```python
# In tests/integration/train_tests.py
# Modify the test methods to supply a unique run_name, e.g.:
train_main(TrainTests.CONFIGS["moe_sparse"] + ["quantization=fp8", "run_name=runner_test_moe_fp8_sparse_matmul"])
```

### github-actions[bot] · 2026-09-12

### 🤖 CI Failure Investigation Report

I have analyzed the recent test failures in the CI pipeline and identified the following:

#### 🔍 What Failed
* **Job/Matrix**: MaxText Package Tests
* **Failing Test**: `tests/post_training/unit/maxtext_engine_test.py::MaxTextTrainingEngineTest::test_max_text_trainer_checkpoint_manager_init`
* **Error**: `AssertionError: Mock call assertion failed due to unexpected/missing 'item_handlers' argument`

#### 🪵 Error Details & Stack Trace
```python
# Mismatch in Mock assertion for CheckpointManager instantiation:
mock_create_mgr.assert_called_once_with(
    directory=mock_config.checkpoint_dir,
    options=ocp.CheckpointManagerOptions(
        save_interval_steps=mock_config.checkpoint_period,
        max_to_keep=mock_config.max_num_checkpoints_to_keep,
        enable_async_checkpointing=mock_config.async_checkpointing,
    ),
)
# AssertionError: CheckpointManager was called with extra/unexpected keyword argument 'item_handlers'
```

#### 💡 Root Cause Analysis & Context
**Confidence:** high (Confirmed Cause)

The failure was a **codebase regression** caused by **PR #5208** ("yixuann-training-engine-support-ocdbt-zarr3"). 

PR #5208 modified `src/maxtext/training_engine/checkpointing.py` to support OCDbt and Zarr3 formats by passing `item_handlers` to `ocp.CheckpointManager`. However, the unit test `test_max_text_trainer_checkpoint_manager_init` in `tests/post_training/unit/maxtext_engine_test.py` was not updated to expect this keyword argument, causing mock assertion failures in the CI run.

#### 🛠️ Recommended Fix
**Note:** This has already been resolved on `main` via **PR #5211** ("Fix CheckpointManager init unit test failure"), which updated the unit test to patch `PyTreeCheckpointHandler`, assert the presence of `item_handlers` in `CheckpointManager`'s mock call, and verify it is instantiated with the expected OCDbt and Zarr3 storage configurations:

```python
  @mock.patch("orbax.checkpoint.PyTreeCheckpointHandler")
  @mock.patch("orbax.checkpoint.CheckpointManager")
  def test_max_text_trainer_checkpoint_manager_init(self, mock_create_mgr, mock_handler):
    mock_config = self.setup_config(
        enable_checkpointing=True,
        checkpoint_storage_use_ocdbt=False,
        checkpoint_storage_use_zarr3=False,
    )

    _ = maxtext_engine.MaxTextTrainingEngine(mock_config)
    mock_create_mgr.assert_called_once_with(
        directory=mock_config.checkpoint_dir,
        options=ocp.CheckpointManagerOptions(
            save_interval_steps=mock_config.checkpoint_period,
            max_to_keep=mock_config.max_num_checkpoints_to_keep,
            enable_async_checkpointing=mock_config.async_checkpointing,
        ),
        item_handlers={
            "model_params": mock_handler.return_value,
            "optimizer_state": mock_handler.return_value,
            "accumulated_metrics": mock_handler.return_value,
            "accumulated_grads": mock_handler.return_value,
        },
    )
    self.assertEqual(mock_handler.call_count, 4)
    mock_handler.assert_has_calls(
        [mock.call(use_ocdbt=False, use_zarr3=False)] * 4,
    )
```
